FINNIFTY / MIDCPNIFTY / NIFTYNXT50 are NOT in Track 1 scope (Track 2 candidates). **Test-class changes:** (a) `TestUniquenessAndDedup` — removed xfail (already used `_idx`); now runs; (b) `TestReadAPI` — rewrote `test_list_in_scope` + `test_list_indices` + `test_count_by_underlying` with 3 of 4 Track 1 indices; `test_list_options` correctly skipped (F&O-only API); (c) `TestInstrumentSnapshot` — rewrote 4 tests with 4 Track 1 indices (SENSEX uses BSE exchange); `test_snapshot_by_underlying` skipped (F&O semantics); (d) `TestInstrumentRunMetadata` — removed xfail (no fixtures used); (e) `TestExpiriesTable` + `TestContractsTable` — changed to `@pytest.mark.skip(reason="Track 2 F&O scope...")` since contracts/expiries tables are F&O-specific by design; (f) `TestIdempotenceStress` — rewrote with 4 Track 1 indices. **`_idx()` helper rewrite:** replaced `exchange_token=26000` (fixed value caused PK collisions when same _idx() called multiple times in a batch) with deterministic per-symbol token map matching real Dhan IDs: `{"NIFTY": 13, "BANKNIFTY": 25, "SENSEX": 51, "INDIAVIX": 21, ...}` with `zlib.crc32()` fallback for unmapped symbols. Helper also accepts `exchange` parameter (default NSE) so SENSEX can be created on BSE per real-world reality. **Test integrity fix:** `TestUniquenessAndDedup::test_verify_integrity_finds_no_duplicates_after_100_upserts` assertion updated from `report.total_rows == 5` → `== 4` (4 Track 1 indices, not 5 mixed). *[Corrected by DEC-P0-014 Agent 3 audit: original entry cited a non-existent method name `TestIdempotenceStress::test_partial_failure_rollback` — Golden Rule #1 hallucination fix.]* **Final test result:** 27 passed, 8 skipped, 0 failed (was 0 passed, 35 xfailed/skipped). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** 2 F&O-specific test classes (`TestExpiriesTable`, `TestContractsTable`) are now `@pytest.mark.skip`-marked rather than rewritten because contracts + expiries tables in the lake schema exist specifically to record F&O contract metadata (strike, expiry, option_type); there is no Track 1 IDX equivalent to test. They will activate when Track 2 lands. **Local verification:** ruff/format/mypy strict/pyright strict ALL GREEN; pytest 1530 passed, 10 skipped.

326. **DEC-CANDLE-RANGE-PICKER-001 — from/to-date candle picker for the per-instrument pull** (operator request 2026-05-30 "for the specified dates dude"). The third of the operator's three /universe asks (after the #204 wipe button + #205 true-8-rps parallel pull). **Gap closed:** `pull_controller.pull_candles` took only a `year` and hardcoded `[Jan 1, Jan 1 next year)`; the operator wants candles for an arbitrary `[from, to]` window. **Change (additive, engine untouched — Golden Rule #3):** (a) new pure, O(1) `resolve_candle_range(from_date, to_date, year, now)` resolves the half-open `[start, end)` UTC window — explicit-range mode (either/both dates, **day-inclusive**, clamped to `[2020-01-01, today]` per the Groww history floor, CLAUDE.md §6) takes priority, else the legacy full-`year` mode; a malformed ISO date or an inverted `from > to` raises `ValueError` (surfaced as the route's 400, DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001); (b) `pull_candles` gains `from_date` / `to_date` kwargs and stamps the resolved `start_date` / `end_date` on `PullCandlesReport` + `to_dict`; the existing `plan_chunks` 30/90/180-day window tiling already covers any range, so the chunk/fetch spine is unchanged (Golden Rule #11 COMMON — no copy-paste); (c) route `POST /api/v1/universe/pull` gains `from_date` / `to_date` query params; (d) `/universe` UI adds two `<input type="date" min="2020-01-01">` pickers next to the pull button — they scope **every** pull path (spot / FUT / CE-PE), so the operator's "spot alone / fut alone / options alone" type-isolation is preserved (one selection = one `groww_symbol`, all 12 timeframes, over the chosen window). **Type-scoping note:** verified already-correct in the current code — `runPull(symbol)` POSTs exactly one symbol and never auto-expands a spot pick into its FUT/options. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the range *resolution* is O(1); the *chunk count* scales with the window width (inherent to the range, not a hot-path regression). `brutex.dashboard.app` stays coverage-excluded (needs FastAPI at runtime); the route is exercised by the `[ui]`-lane smoke test. **Local verification:** ruff format + ruff check GREEN; mypy --strict GREEN on `pull_controller.py`; `pull_controller.py` 100% line + 100% branch (88 stmts / 10 branches, 0 miss) across 32 unit tests incl. 12 `resolve_candle_range` cases (modes, clamps, inversion, malformed-ISO) + 3 `pull_candles` range cases.

327. **DEC-DB-QUERY-001 — structured query (filter rows + pick columns) + candle/OHLCV viewer on `/db`** (operator request 2026-05-30 "why queryign is not available … where is the historical candle pull data table"). **Gaps closed:** (1) the `/db` viewer was paginate-only — `sample_rows` ran `SELECT * … LIMIT ? OFFSET ?` with no WHERE / column-select / ORDER BY, so an operator could not see "particular rows or columns alone"; (2) pulled candle OHLCV data lives as **Parquet** at `<lake>/bars/<exch>/<seg>/<symbol>/<interval>/<yyyy>/<mm>.parquet` (parquet_writer schema: `timestamp, open, high, low, close, volume, open_interest`) and had **no UI surface at all** — the `/db` `KNOWN_TABLES` allowlist only knows the 5 DuckDB metadata tables. **Change (additive, read-only, engine untouched — Golden Rule #3):** all real logic in `brutex/dashboard/db_view.py` (CLAUDE.md §11 COMMON), routes are thin coverage-excluded wrappers. (a) `query_table(conn, table, *, columns, filters, order_by, descending, limit, offset)` — structured query with **safe-by-construction** identifier handling: table ∈ `KNOWN_TABLES`, every projected/filter/order column validated against the table's REAL introspected columns (`list_columns`), operators from a fixed `ALLOWED_OPERATORS` map (`eq/ne/lt/le/gt/ge/like/contains`), and **every comparison value parameter-bound** (`?`) — zero SQL-injection surface; `total` reflects the filtered count. (b) `list_candle_datasets(lake_root)` discovers `(exchange, segment, symbol, interval)` sets from the bars tree (skips foreign/empty dirs; missing `bars/` → `[]`). (c) `query_candles(conn, lake_root, …)` reads the Parquet lake via DuckDB `read_parquet(?)` over the partition glob — coordinates validated against the partition allowlists (`VALID_EXCHANGES/SEGMENTS/INTERVALS` + a strict `_SYMBOL_RE` + `..` reject so a coordinate can never traverse outside `<lake>/bars`), `start`/`end` strict inclusive ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` bounds matched against the bar's **UTC calendar date** (`("timestamp" AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::DATE` — session-`TimeZone`-independent, so the operator's Asia/Kolkata box drops/adds no boundary bars; Golden Rule #7), `LIMIT`-bounded + scoped to one `(symbol, interval)` (DuckDB additionally skips row-groups via min/max stats — engine cost, not directory pruning); an empty/not-yet-pulled set returns an empty page instead of raising. (d) routes `GET /api/v1/db/columns/{name}`, `/api/v1/db/query/{name}`, `/api/v1/db/candles/datasets`, `/api/v1/db/candles` (ValueError → HTTP 400). (e) `/db` UI gains a column-checkbox + dynamic filter-row + sort query bar for the 5 tables, and a 📈 **candles** dataset/date-range/column/sort browser over the Parquet lake; all server strings `esc()`-escaped (XSS). **O(1)/worst-case (operator standing requirement):** identifier validation is O(1) hash-set membership; a page read is `LIMIT`-bounded + partition-pruned (DuckDB prunes by the path glob — never scans the full lake); every worst case is covered by a test with a real error (unknown table/column/operator/order-column, empty column tuple, inverted/malformed coords, malformed ISO date, embedded-`"` identifier, foreign/empty candle dirs, not-yet-pulled set). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the row *scan* under a filter is DuckDB-cost (not O(1) in the row count) — bounded by the page `LIMIT` for the returned set + the engine's own indexing, not a BruteX hot path; `brutex.dashboard.app` stays coverage-excluded, the routes are proven by a `[ui]`-lane TestClient smoke. **4-agent hostile review (CLAUDE.md §9):** hot-path + security + cite/path SAFE; correctness BLOCKED on a HIGH (date bounds cast under the session `TimeZone`) → **fixed** (UTC-anchored `::DATE` bounds) + 2 MEDIUMs fixed (strict `YYYY-MM-DD` parse; `contains` now escapes LIKE `%`/`_` for literal-substring match, with a regression test each incl. an Asia/Kolkata session-tz test). **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN on `db_view.py`; `db_view.py` **100% line + 100% branch** (101 stmts / 48 branches, 0 miss) across 81 unit tests; end-to-end TestClient smoke of all 4 routes (columns / eq-filter / bad-op→400 / dataset-discovery / candle projection / bad-interval→400) GREEN.

328. **DEC-PULL-VISIBILITY-RESILIENCE-001 — surface swallowed per-chunk fetch errors + bound the master-CSV download so the expiries phase can't hang** (operator live run 2026-05-30: "why this fetch all expiries is hanging … why it is not even pulling any data"). Two distinct LIVE-path bugs the operator hit (both in `# pragma: no cover` Groww code that CI cannot exercise — network-blocked). **Bug 1 — silent 100%-failed pull:** clicking "Pull spot — all timeframes" on NSE-NIFTY logged `universe.pull.done completed=0 failed=31` — every chunk failed but the **real Groww reason was discarded**: `fetch_loop.run_fetch` caught the vendor exception, recorded `ChunkStatus.FAILED`, bumped a counter, and **never logged `exc`**, so a systematic rejection (most likely `segment=CASH` being wrong for an INDEX — CLAUDE.md §6 marks the index symbol/segment "Phase 0b verification pending" = UNVERIFIED) was indistinguishable from an empty range. **Fix:** new `_log_chunk_failure` emits one `ingest.chunk.failed` warning per quarantined chunk carrying `symbol/interval/start/end/outcome/error_type/error/retries_exhausted` at BOTH failure sites (terminal + transient-retries-exhausted) — the swallowed reason now prints to the JSONL log + `/logs` (DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001 extended to the per-chunk loop). The `/universe` pull panel now shows "✕ N chunk(s) failed … see /logs" when `total_failed>0 && total_completed==0` instead of the misleading "∅ no data returned". **Bug 2 — "Fetch all expiries" hangs forever, button stuck:** the phase logs `universe.expiries.master.refreshing` then never reaches `universe.expiries.start`; the worker is blocked inside `InstrumentMasterFetcher.refresh() → http_get → urlopen(...).read()`. `urlopen(timeout=30)` is a **per-socket-op** timeout, not a total deadline — a slow-trickle server keeps each read under 30s while the whole multi-MB transfer stalls for minutes, so `_start_phase._run`'s `finally` (which releases the single-flight lock) never runs and the button latches disabled. **Fix:** new pure, injectable `read_with_deadline(resp, *, deadline_s=60, max_bytes=256MiB, monotonic)` reads to EOF in 1-MiB chunks bounded by a TOTAL wall-clock deadline + size cap, raising the typed `InstrumentDownloadError` on a stall — which the existing `_start_phase` `except`→`universe.phase.failed` log + `finally`→lock-release already handle, so a stalled download now un-sticks the button + surfaces the reason instead of hanging (closes the DEC-PULL-TIMEOUT-DEFER-001 deferral for the master download). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** I could NOT reproduce the live Groww rejection from the sandbox (network blocked + the live wiring is `# pragma: no cover`), so this PR makes the failure **observable** (the operator's next run prints the exact `ingest.chunk.failed` reason, pinpointing the precise root cause) rather than blindly changing the index segment — a guessed segment flip would violate Golden Rule #1 (no unverified change). The actual index-segment correction is a tracked follow-up gated on that surfaced error. **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN; `fetch_loop.py` **100% line+branch** (68 stmts / 14 branches) across 14 tests incl. 3 new visibility tests (terminal + transient-exhausted reason logged; success/no-data log nothing); `instruments.py` **100% line+branch** (124 stmts / 20 branches) across 24 tests incl. 3 new `read_with_deadline` tests (EOF, deadline-exceeded, size-cap).

329. **DEC-PULL-CANDLE-TIME-FORMAT-001 — `get_historical_candles` was sent an INT EPOCH; the SDK documents an IST `"yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss"` STRING → every candle pull rejected** (follow-up to the #328 visibility unlock; root-caused 2026-05-30 by reading the installed `growwapi` wheel source — verifiable without the live API). **The actual root cause** of the operator's 100%-failed NSE-NIFTY pull (and it is **NOT** index-specific — it breaks index AND stock pulls): `client._build_request` passed `start_time` / `end_time` via `_to_epoch(...)` = an **integer epoch**, but the HARD-VERIFIED SDK docstring for `GrowwAPI.get_historical_candles` states `start_time (str): The start time in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format` (the SDK forwards the value verbatim as a query param). The prior code comment claimed "epoch is also accepted (backtesting doc Request Schema)" — an **UNVERIFIED** claim contradicted by the wheel; live Groww rejected every chunk (`completed=0 failed=N`). The SDK also has **no index segment** (only `SEGMENT_CASH/FNO/COMMODITY/CURRENCY`), so `segment=CASH` for an index was a red herring — `CASH` is the only option. **Fix:** `_to_epoch` → `_to_groww_time(iso_ts) -> str` returns the documented `"yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss"` string in **IST** (Asia/Kolkata) — symmetric with the response side, which `parser._parse_timestamp` already reads back as IST market time (CLAUDE.md §6). Three independent, source-verifiable signals align: (1) the live 100% failure with epoch; (2) the SDK docstring's documented string format; (3) the parser's established IST convention. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the format + IST tz match the SDK's documented contract + the parser's symmetry (high confidence, source-verified — NOT a blind guess), but I still could not hit the live Groww API from the sandbox (network blocked), so final live confirmation is the operator's next run; #328's `ingest.chunk.failed` visibility will surface any residual error (e.g. a wrong index symbol form) if this is not the full cure. **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN on `client.py`; `client.py` **100% line+branch** (75 stmts / 10 branches, 0 miss) across 21 tests incl. the updated request-mapping assertion (`start_time=="2021-01-04 14:45:00"`, the IST of `09:15 UTC`) + 2 new `_to_groww_time` tests (tz-aware-UTC→IST, naive-assumed-UTC).

330. **DEC-CANDLE-PARSE-RESILIENCE-001 — accept an index's null `volume` + per-row candle resilience (THE blocking bug; corrects the #329 root-cause framing)** (operator live run 2026-05-30, after #328's visibility fix printed the exact reason + the operator pasted the official Groww backtesting docs). **Reconciliation with #329:** the operator's live run used the epoch format (main, pre-#329) and the requests **SUCCEEDED** — Groww returned real candle rows — so the integer-epoch time was **NOT** the blocking bug (#329's "every candle pull rejected" framing is corrected here: the IST `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` change is a doc-alignment correctness improvement, not the cure). #328's `ingest.chunk.failed` log revealed the TRUE cause: `corrupt candle in 'NSE-NIFTY': unparseable volume: None` (index) and `corrupt candle in 'NSE-ADANIENSOL': unparseable open price: None` (a null row) — i.e. the requests work, our **parser** rejected the response. **Confirmed against the official `groww.in/trade-api/docs/curl/backtesting` candle response:** row shape `[ts, o, h, l, c, volume, oi]`, `open interest … null for others`, volume populated for the equity example but **null for spot indices** (CLAUDE.md §12 "no volume on spot"). **Two fixes (parse/persist path; engine untouched):** (a) `parser.parse_candle_row` maps a **null `volume` → 0** (a valid index bar) instead of raising — this alone unblocks the 3 brute-force indices (NIFTY/BANKNIFTY/SENSEX); OHLC stay strict. (b) `persist.PersistingFetcher.fetch_chunk` is **per-row resilient**: it loops + **skips** a corrupt row (the old single list-comprehension let ONE bad row `bad_request`-quarantine the entire 30/90/180-day chunk) and emits one `candle.rows.quarantined` warning per chunk (`quarantined`/`kept`/`sample_error`); an all-corrupt chunk returns `[]` → `NO_DATA`. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the index null-volume fix is doc-confirmed + reproduced via the operator's live `ingest.chunk.failed`. The stock `open: None` rows are now per-row-skipped + counted, so the next run reports exactly how many null rows `NSE-ADANIENSOL` returns (diagnoses sparse-2026 vs all-null) instead of nuking the window. Live `from_env`/network stays `# pragma: no cover`; the pure parse/persist logic is 100% covered. **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN; `parser.py` + `persist.py` + `client.py` **100% line+branch** (45+47+75 stmts / 12+12+10 branches, 0 miss) across 55 unit tests incl. null-volume parse, per-row skip (mixed + all-corrupt with logged count + sample), index-null-volume-persists-zero.

331. **DEC-CANDLE-EMPTY-BAR-001 — a no-data bar (null/empty/zero OHLC) is a GRACEFUL skip, not a quarantine** (operator follow-up 2026-05-30: "even for some stocks open price can be null or empty or zero … if there is no OHLCV data for anything we should never ever worry about it"). Sharpens #330: a no-trade / halted / not-yet-listed / illiquid period legitimately returns a bar with **null / empty-string / zero** OHLC — there is no price to store, and it must be dropped *quietly* (expected), distinct from a partially-malformed bar (some prices real, some broken) which stays a logged quarantine. **Fixes:** (a) `parser` gains `_is_absent` (None / empty / whitespace string) + `_has_no_price` (absent OR `Decimal==0` — a traded Indian instrument's price is never 0, tick ≥ 0.05) and a new `EmptyCandleRow(CandleParseError)`; `parse_candle_row` raises `EmptyCandleRow` when **all four** OHLC have no price (graceful), while a *partial* null (e.g. only `open` absent) still falls through to the strict `_to_price` → `CandleParseError` (a real data-quality warning). Absent `volume`/`oi` now also tolerate empty strings (→ 0 / None). (b) `persist.fetch_chunk` catches `EmptyCandleRow` **before** `CandleParseError` (subclass-ordering) and counts it as `empty`: a chunk with only no-data bars logs `candle.rows.empty_skipped` at **info** (not a scary warning) and settles `NO_DATA`; a chunk with any malformed row logs the single `candle.rows.quarantined` **warning** carrying both `quarantined` + `empty` counts. Net: index/stock no-trade bars never fail or alarm; genuinely-broken bars still surface; good bars always persist. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** "zero == no price" holds for Indian spot equities/indices (never priced at 0); if a future market allowed a legitimate 0 price this would need revisiting (out of Track 1 scope). Live wiring stays `# pragma: no cover`; the pure logic is 100% covered. **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN; `parser.py` **100% line+branch** (57 stmts / 16 branches) + `persist.py` **100%** (52 / 14) across 43 tests incl. all-null/all-empty/all-zero → EmptyCandleRow, partial-null → malformed (not empty), empty-string volume→0 / oi→None, and persist empty-only→info+NO_DATA / good+empty→keep-good+info / corrupt+empty→warning-with-both-counts.

332. **DEC-STORE-LOCK-LEAK-FIX-001 — `/db` `list_columns` leaked the store lock → "Fetch all expiries" deadlocked for 26 minutes** (operator live run 2026-05-30: "why still fetch all expiries failing"). **Concurrency / race-condition worst case** introduced by DEC-DB-QUERY-001 (#327) and missed because the tests were single-threaded (a re-entrant `RLock` hides the leak within one thread). **Root cause:** `InstrumentStore.connection` returns a `_LockedReadConn` whose contract is "acquire the store lock in `execute()`, release it only in `fetchone()`/`fetchall()`." The new `db_view.list_columns` read `cursor.description` (column names) and returned **without fetching** → the store `RLock` was acquired and **never released**. In production the `/db` page calls `list_columns` on load (a request thread), so the lock leaked; the background "Fetch all expiries" phase then hit `InstrumentStore.upsert_many` (`with self._lock`) on a DIFFERENT thread and **blocked forever** — the live log shows `GET /api/v1/db/columns/instruments` then `master.refreshing` then 26 minutes of nothing until restart. **Two-layer fix (Correctness > Reliability):** (a) `list_columns` now calls `cursor.fetchall()` (the `LIMIT 0` result is empty) after reading `.description`, releasing the lock — harmless on a raw cursor. (b) **Defense-in-depth:** `_LockedReadConn` is hardened so a description-only reader can NEVER leak again — an idempotent `_release` (tracks `_held`), a public `close()`, and full context-manager support (`__enter__`/`__exit__`), so `with store.connection.execute(...) as cur:` always frees the lock even with no fetch, and the `execute()` error path now routes through `_release` too. `execute()` also drops any prior hold first (`self._release()` at entry), so even a re-execute on the same view without an intervening fetch can never leak an extra acquisition (hostile-review LOW closed). **Testing (the worst-case the operator demanded):** 7 new CONCURRENCY tests — `list_columns` releases the lock for a *different* thread; `with`-block release without fetch; idempotent double-`close`; `fetchall` release; execute-error release; re-execute-without-fetch no-leak; and an **end-to-end deadlock regression** (a `/db` read followed by a store WRITE from another thread completes within 5 s, mirroring the live expiries hang). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the deadlock is reproduced + fixed deterministically in a unit test (no live network needed — it is a pure threading bug); `db_view.py` stays **100% line+branch** (103 stmts / 48 branches, 0 miss); the new `_LockedReadConn` paths are exercised by the 6 concurrency tests (CI's full-suite coverage gate measures `instrument_store.py` to 100%). **Local verification:** ruff + mypy --strict GREEN; 87 db unit tests pass incl. the 6 new concurrency tests.

333. **DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-8RPS-001 — the universe pull was latency-bound at 2.75 r/s under an 8 r/s ceiling; size the worker pool to saturate the bucket + add achieved-r/s telemetry** (operator live run 2026-05-30: "why the f*** 2.75 rps … I clearly told you to make it 8 rps … ensure me with guarantee"). **Root cause (NOT a misconfigured rate):** the adaptive limiter's ceiling was already 8 r/s and the run took **0 rate-limit hits**, yet the "Fetch all expiries" phase achieved only **2.75 r/s** (1652 cells in 601 s). The pull is **latency-bound, not rate-bound**: `PoolRunParallel` ran the documented `DEFAULT_MAX_WORKERS = 8`, and (verified) `RemintGuard.call` runs the thread-safe SDK call WITHOUT a lock, so 8 workers ran truly concurrently — but each `get_expiries` round-trip takes ~2.9 s, so 8 ÷ 2.9 ≈ 2.75 calls/s and the 8 r/s token bucket was **never fed** (the bucket said "you may do 8/s"; only 8 calls could ever be in flight). The stale cli comment "guard.call serializes the shared SDK" reinforced the misread and is corrected. **Fix (Little's law — `in_flight = rate × latency`):** new pure `parallel.workers_to_saturate(max_rps, latency_budget_s=3.0, *, cap=32)` sizes the pool to `ceil(max_rps × latency_budget)` (≈ 24 at 8 r/s × 3 s), clamped to `[1, MAX_FANOUT=32]`; both live phases now build `PoolRunParallel(workers_to_saturate(AdaptiveRateLimiterConfig.from_env().max_rps))`. **Rate-limit safety (Golden Rule #6) is UNCHANGED:** the token bucket still caps the *rate* at ≤ `max_rps` regardless of fan-out — more workers only fill idle network latency, they never raise the per-second cap; if Groww 429s, the adaptive limiter still descends to the 4 r/s floor (a safety feature, not a regression). **Guarantee + proof (the operator's "ensure me"):** new pure, O(1)-space, thread-safe `phase_throughput.PhaseThroughput` wraps each call in `track()` and the `universe.expiries.done` / `universe.contracts.done` lines now carry `target_rps`, `achieved_rps`, `latency_mean/min/max_s`, and `peak_in_flight` — so every run *prints* where the rate landed vs the ceiling (`peak_in_flight == workers` ⇒ raise fan-out; lower ⇒ bucket/worklist bound). Impact: the ~19 k-job contracts phase goes from ~115 min (2.75 r/s) toward ~40 min (8 r/s). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** I cannot promise Groww will *sustain* 8 r/s — the backtesting bucket cap is UNVERIFIED (CLAUDE.md §6); the guarantee is (a) the bucket targets 8 and is never exceeded, (b) the pool is sized to saturate it, (c) telemetry proves the achieved r/s each run. The `latency_budget_s=3.0` default is set from THIS run's ~2.9 s observation; the new telemetry measures the real value so it can be tuned from evidence (the operator's chosen "instrument-first, then tune" path). The live phase wiring stays `# pragma: no cover` (live SDK + threads); the pure `workers_to_saturate` + `PhaseThroughput` logic is **100% line+branch**. **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict + pyright (incl. tests) GREEN; `parallel.py` **100%** (31 stmts / 6 branches) + `phase_throughput.py` **100%** (62 stmts / 4 branches, 0 miss) across 23 unit tests (8 `workers_to_saturate` incl. both OR-branch + cap + ceil-rounding + floor-to-one cases; 8 `PhaseThroughput` incl. empty-phase 0/0, zero-wall, mean/min/max, failed-call-counts, peak-in-flight high-water + sequential-no-reinflate, and `as_log_fields` rounding). **Builds on:** DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-CONCURRENCY-001 (#322, the lock-free shared bucket) · DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-PARALLEL-001 (#319, the pool + bucket) · DEC-ADAPTIVE-RATE-LIMITER-001 (#249) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1)) / Rule 6 (rate safety) / §10 (honest limits).

334. **DEC-PULL-RETRY-OFFBYONE-001 — the fetch loop crashed the WHOLE pull on the first transient error: `ValueError: retry_count must be >= 1, got 0`** (operator live run 2026-05-31: a contract pull surfaced `pull failed: ValueError: retry_count must be >= 1, got 0`). **Root cause (off-by-one):** `fetch_loop.run_fetch` initialised `retry_count = 0` and, on the first transient outcome, called `sleep_fn(delay_fn(retry_count))` BEFORE incrementing — passing `0` to `backoff_delay`, which is **1-based** (`retry_count <= 0` raises `ValueError`, `retry.py:225`). So the instant ANY transient error occurred (rate-limit / timeout / generic `VendorAPIException`), the resilient retry path raised instead of backing off, and `run_ingest` propagated the `ValueError` up through `pull_controller.pull_candles` → the dashboard returned `400 pull failed`. The masking made it look like a brutex bug when the real upstream signal was the vendor's `"Invalid start time. Start time cannot be in the future"` (a `GENERIC_API_ERROR` → transient → retry path → crash). **Fix (one-line reorder):** increment `retry_count` BEFORE computing the delay, so `delay_fn` receives a 1-based count (`1, 2, …`) and the first backoff is the schedule's 2 s slot — never 0. Retry bounding (`retry_count < max_retries` → exactly `max_retries` retries then QUARANTINE) is unchanged; transient errors now back off + retry as designed, and a deterministic vendor rejection settles `FAILED` with its REAL reason logged (DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001) instead of crashing the run. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this fixes the CRASH; it does NOT change that a future-dated request window is still rejected by Groww (the separate `start time … in the future` condition — the pull now QUARANTINEs that chunk and surfaces the reason rather than aborting; right-sizing the contract pull date-window to its real listing range is a tracked follow-up, not this entry). **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `fetch_loop.py` **100% line+branch** (68 stmts / 14 branches, 0 miss) across 18 unit tests incl. 2 new regressions (`test_first_retry_passes_one_based_count_to_delay_fn` asserts the recorded delay counts are `[1, 2]` never `0`; `test_real_backoff_delay_does_not_crash_on_first_retry` drives the loop with the PRODUCTION `backoff_delay` — the stub previously hid the bug by ignoring its arg). **Builds on:** DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001 (#320) · DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-PARALLEL-001 (#319, `retry_call`) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 7 (idempotence) / §4 (no bare except) / §10 (honest limits).

335. **DEC-DASHBOARD-WIPE-REMOVE-001 — RETIRE the dashboard "Delete all ingested data" button + its full stack (button, route, view-guards, store method, lake-truncate core)** (operator decree 2026-05-31: "remove this delete all ingested data button and its design functionality and its implementation as well"). **Retires DEC-DASHBOARD-WIPE-001 (#324).** Removed end-to-end: (a) the `/universe` danger-zone block + `🗑 Delete all ingested data` button + `#wipemsg` + the `wipeDataBtn` JS handler in `universe.html`; (b) the `POST /api/v1/db/wipe` route + the `WipeConfirmIn` body model in `dashboard/app.py` (and the now-unused `Request` import); (c) `dashboard/wipe_view.py` (the HTTP-side guards) and `data/lake_wipe.py` (the `LAKE_DATA_TABLES` truncate core) — both deleted; (d) `InstrumentStore.wipe_lake_tables` + its `lake_wipe` import in `instrument_store.py`; (e) the two now-dead test modules `tests/unit/test_wipe_view.py` + `tests/unit/test_lake_wipe.py`. **Kept (out of scope):** the file-level `brutex wipe` / `make wipe` CLI (`brutex/wipe.py`, DEC-DATA-WIPE-001 #322) remains the supported clean-slate path — the retired danger-zone copy already pointed operators there. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the other `/db` viewer routes (`tables`, `columns`, `query`, `candles`, `table`) and the `/db` page are untouched and verified present; the brute-force ENGINE is unaffected (Golden Rule #3 scope lock). `app.py` + `instrument_store.py` are coverage-OMITTED glue (pyproject `[tool.coverage.run] omit`), so removing the route/method does not move the coverage gate; the deleted `lake_wipe`/`wipe_view` units (and their 24 tests) leave no orphaned references (repo-wide grep clean). **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `build_app(DashboardState())` constructs with the wipe route ABSENT and the 5 other `/db` routes present; full `tests/unit` suite **2567 passed**. **Builds on / retires:** DEC-DASHBOARD-WIPE-001 (#324, the feature now removed) · DEC-DATA-WIPE-001 (#322, the surviving CLI wipe) · CLAUDE.md §3 (scope) / §11 (COMMON — one wipe path, not two) / §10 (honest limits).

336. **DEC-PULL-FUTURE-WINDOW-001 — the year/default date-window mode built an UNCLAMPED future window → vendor rejected the first future chunk; clamp every mode to `[2020-floor, today]`** (operator follow-up 2026-05-31: "cover all kinds of extreme worst case scenarios … I will never consider all these cases"). **Root cause (the REAL upstream signal behind #334):** `pull_controller.resolve_candle_range`'s "no dates given" branch returned `[year-01-01, (year+1)-01-01)` with **no clamp** — so the current year (or an explicit current/future `year=`) produced a window whose tail ran past today. The live run pulled `year=2026` on 2026-05-31 → `[2026-01-01, 2027-01-01)`; Groww rejected the first future chunk with `"Invalid start time. Start time cannot be in the future"`. The explicit-`from`/`to` branch right below it ALREADY clamped to `[GROWW_HISTORY_FLOOR=2020-01-01, today]` — the year branch was the one unclamped path. **Worst-case-coverage failure:** the bug shipped because `test_no_dates_no_year_uses_current_utc_year` *asserted* the buggy `[2026-01-01, 2027-01-01)` — a test that pinned the future window as "correct". **Fix:** the year/default branch now builds a `(start_date, end_date)` date pair that falls through the SAME clamp as mode 1; the current year stops at today, a wholly-future or pre-2020 year raises "nothing to pull" (never a future request to the vendor). The pinning test was corrected + 4 edge tests added (current-year-clamp, past-year-full, future-year-raises, pre-floor-year-raises). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this stops the crash AND the wasted future request. It does NOT make a wrong-year contract pull (e.g. a 2020-expiry option pulled under year=2026) return data — that window now legitimately resolves to a non-overlapping range and settles NO_DATA gracefully; deriving a contract's window from its parsed expiry/listing range is a separate tracked follow-up. **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `test_dashboard_pull_controller.py` **56 passed** incl. the 4 new clamp edge cases. **Builds on:** DEC-PULL-RETRY-OFFBYONE-001 (#334) · DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-2020-001 (the floor + today clamp on mode 1) · CLAUDE.md §6 (Groww depth from 2020) / §10 (honest limits).

337. **DEC-PULL-CHUNK-CONTAINMENT-001 — a NON-vendor exception in the fetch loop crashed the whole pull; contain it per-chunk (quarantine + error-log) instead** (operator follow-up 2026-05-31, same "cover every nook and corner" decree). **Root cause (the crash-CLASS behind #334, not just the one instance):** `fetch_loop.run_fetch`'s `except Exception` block called `classify_exception(exc)`, which RAISES `UnknownExceptionError` for any exception not in `BROKER_EXCEPTION_CLASSIFICATION` (a programmer bug, a corrupt watermark row, a chunk-planning slip, …). That raise propagated straight out of `run_fetch` → `run_ingest` → `pull_candles` → a crashed route — the exact failure shape as the off-by-one `ValueError`, just from a different trigger. So fixing #334 alone left the door open: any future unexpected error would again kill a multi-thousand-chunk pull. **Fix (blast-radius containment, Golden Rule #8 + #10):** wrap `classify_exception`; on `UnknownExceptionError`, log `ingest.chunk.unexpected_error` at **error** level with the real `error_type` + `error` + chunk key, record the chunk `FAILED`, and **continue** — one bad chunk never aborts the others (it is loudly surfaced, never silently swallowed). Catastrophic `BaseException` (KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit) is unaffected (the block catches `Exception` only). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this contains exceptions reaching the per-chunk loop; it does NOT wrap the pre-loop set-up (watermark load, chunk planning) — those still surface to the route, which is acceptable (they are not per-chunk and are far rarer). This is deliberate scoping, not full coverage of every conceivable raise site. **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `fetch_loop.py` **100% line+branch** (74 stmts / 14 branches, 0 miss) across 19 tests incl. 3 new containment tests (non-vendor quarantines-not-crashes, error-log carries real type/message, one-bad-chunk-doesn't-abort-the-rest). **Builds on:** DEC-PULL-RETRY-OFFBYONE-001 (#334) · DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001 (#320) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 8 (audit) / §4 (no bare except — this is a SPECIFIC `UnknownExceptionError` catch) / §10 (honest limits).

338. **DEC-DB-CANDLE-IST-DISPLAY-001 — the `/db` candle viewer showed bar times with a noisy `+05:30` offset; render a clean IST wall-clock** (operator 2026-05-31: "why +5.30 weird timestamp … it should be clean"). The lake stores bar time as a UTC `TIMESTAMPTZ` (`parquet_writer` `time_zone="UTC"`); DuckDB hands it back as a tz-aware `datetime`, and `db_view._json_safe` stringified it → `2026-05-29 09:00:00+05:30` (the operator's session runs Asia/Kolkata). **Fix:** `query_candles` now routes the `timestamp` column through a new `_format_candle_timestamp` that converts to IST **explicitly** (via `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo("Asia/Kolkata")`, not the ambient session `TimeZone`) and `strftime`s `"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"` — so a bar at `03:30Z` renders `2026-05-29 09:00:00` (clean, offset-free) identically whether the server runs in UTC (CI) or IST (the operator's box). Only the candle viewer's `timestamp` column is touched; every other cell still goes through `_json_safe`, and the date-range FILTER stays on the UTC calendar date (Golden Rule #7 — same input → same rows, session-tz-independent). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this is a DISPLAY change only — the stored UTC instant is unchanged, and IST is the correct market wall-clock for NSE/BSE (whose 09:15–15:30 IST session maps to the same UTC calendar date, so no date-boundary surprise). **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `db_view.py` **100% line+branch** (110 stmts / 50 branches, 0 miss) across the db-query + db-view suites incl. 4 new tests (tz-aware→IST, naive-datetime kept, non-datetime fallback, and an end-to-end `query_candles` assert that the rendered stamps carry no `+`/`T`). **Builds on:** DEC-DB-QUERY-001 (the candle viewer) · DEC-DB-VIEWER-001 · CLAUDE.md §7 (microstructure IST hours) / §10 (honest limits).

339. **DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 — LOCK "1-minute is the only timeframe fetched from Groww; all other TFs are deterministically resampled from 1m" + record that the live pull currently VIOLATES it** (operator requirements-gathering 2026-05-31: "we can rely only on the one-min timeframe … groww just appends broker-level executed prices, not NSE-direct … they don't backfill or back-adjust … that too in backtesting historical fetch nothing is curated"). **This re-affirms the existing spec, it does NOT change it:** `TRACK1_DATA_INGEST_DESIGN.md` §3.1–§3.3 already says "Native fetched = 1m + 1d; the 19 derived intraday TFs are resampled from the 1m base … Track 1 fetches ONLY 1m". The operator decision adds the **vendor-trust rationale** to that rule (now §3.3): Groww historical candles are its OWN broker-executed-trade tape — NOT reconciled to the NSE/BSE-direct consolidated feed and NOT back-adjusted (unlike Dhan, which reconstructs every minute from the official feed); only the 1m series is a faithful record, so Groww's NATIVE 5/15/25/60-min aggregations are untrusted and must never be ingested. **Implementation drift recorded (the actionable part):** the live pull fetches Groww's **12 native intervals** (`ALL_GROWW_INTERVALS` in `brutex/adapters/groww/chunks.py` = `1m,2m,3m,5m,10m,15m,30m,1h,4h,1d,1w,1mo`) directly — contradicting the spec. The resampler already exists (`brutex/feed/resampler.py`, `brutex/data/tf_resampler.py`, `brutex/data/timeframes.py`), so the corrective follow-up is: ingest **1m-only** (+ `1d` for PDH/PDL anchors), resample to the full derived TF set, add a 1m gap/completeness guard against the 09:15–15:30 session grid, and shrink the per-pull API calls ~12×. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #1 + #10):** the "broker tape, no NSE backfill" characterisation is the OPERATOR's domain knowledge, NOT a Groww-published fact — CLAUDE.md §6 still carries "Groww-vs-NSE drift: UNQUANTIFIED — Phase 0b probe will measure", which this decision is the motivation for. The 3 brute-force INDEXES are index-dissemination values (not traded prints), so the tape concern bites hardest on the single-stock/option lake, where null/zero illiquid-minute bars are already handled (DEC-CANDLE-EMPTY-BAR-001 / DEC-CANDLE-PARSE-RESILIENCE-001). **No code in this entry** — it is the locked decision + spec note the operator requested ("record as a locked decision + spec note"); the 1m-only ingest change is a separate authorised PR. **Builds on:** the 21-TF model (decision #7, 15→21 TFs) · DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 (Groww adapter) · DEC-CANDLE-EMPTY-BAR-001 (#331) · CLAUDE.md §1 (1m base + deterministic resample) / §6 (drift unquantified) / §10 (honest limits).

340. **DEC-LAKE-MERGE-WRITE-001 — the lake writer is now MERGE-on-write (union by timestamp), fixing the chunk-straddles-month `BAD_REQUEST` partition-conflict flood** (operator live runs 2026-05-31, after #339's 1m-only landed: re-pulling NSE-NIFTY/BANKNIFTY 1m showed ~38 `lake write failed … partition … exists with different content` errors — only on the month-boundary chunks). **Root cause (NOT vendor instability — corrects the earlier #214 framing):** fetch chunks are 30/90-day windows that straddle month boundaries, but lake partitions are monthly (`…/1minute/2020/01.parquet`). `sink.append` groups a chunk's bars by `(year,month)` and `parquet_writer.write` was **write-once content-addressed** — so a month touched by two chunks (e.g. a chunk ending mid-month + the next chunk starting at that day) had its full-month partition **overwritten with a partial slice**, and the byte-hash mismatch was rejected. The deterministic fingerprint: only chunks whose START fell on a month's tail (Jan 31, Mar 31, Jun 29, …) failed, conflicting on that start-month — proving a partial-write, not random vendor drift (even **1m** failed, which killed the vendor-instability hypothesis). **Fix:** `ParquetWriter.write` now MERGES — when the partition exists it reads the stored bars (`_read_existing_bars`, reconstructing Decimals via shortest-round-trip `Decimal(str(float))` so an unchanged bar re-renders byte-identically), UNIONS the incoming bars keyed by timestamp (**O(1) dict dedup per bar**), and rewrites the full month only if ≥1 new timestamp was added. A timestamp already stored **identically** is a silent no-op; a SAME timestamp with **different** OHLCV raises `WriterError` (`_bar_values_equal`, numeric `Decimal` ==) — a *genuine* data conflict, never silently overwritten. Net: partial months accumulate, re-pulls are idempotent (added==0 → no rewrite, byte-stable), and only real vendor drift surfaces. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the merge is O(1) per-bar (dict insert) + an O(n·log n) sort over the month's bars (bounded ~7.5k for 1m, not a hot path); `parquet_writer.py` is coverage-OMITTED (needs `polars`), so the 100% gate is unaffected — the new paths are covered by `tests/integration/test_data_lake_real.py` (real Parquet round-trip). The float→Decimal round-trip is safe for ≤4-decimal Indian prices (shortest-repr recovery); a hypothetical >15-sig-digit price is out of scope. **Local verification:** ruff + ruff format + mypy --strict GREEN; `tests/integration/test_data_lake_real.py` **31 passed** incl. 3 new (disjoint-days-accumulate = the straddle scenario, overlap-identical-is-noop = idempotent re-pull byte-stable, same-ts-different-value-raises = true conflict); full `tests/unit` **2577 passed**. **Builds on:** DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339, the 1m-only pull that exposed it) · DEC-CANDLE-EMPTY-BAR-001 (#331) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1) per write) / Rule 7 (idempotence) / §10 (honest limits).


341. **DEC-GROWW-DHAN-REVERT-001 — revert the Track 1 backtesting data source Groww → Dhan; remove everything Groww, rebuild on Dhan v2 REST** (operator directive 2026-05-31: "remove everything entirely related to groww … take these dhan docs … you can directly take the code … which we have already implemented in tickvault"). **Supersedes** DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 (#— / ADR 0001, the 2026-05-26 Dhan→Groww pivot); new **ADR `docs/adr/0002-groww-to-dhan-revert.md`**. **Verified-not-assumed (Golden Rule #1):** the tag `v-pre-groww-pivot` that ADR 0001 + CLAUDE.md §6 claim preserves the old Dhan code **does not exist**, and this 50-commit clone holds **no Dhan object in any reachable ref** (`git rev-list --all --objects | grep -i dhan` → empty) — so the revert is a **from-scratch rebuild, not a `git revert`**. **Scope:** Track 1 backtesting **Data APIs only** (live execution / orders / WebSocket stay OUT, CLAUDE.md §1). **Verified Dhan facts locked under new `docs/dhan-ref/`** (01 rate-limits, 02 auth, 05 historical, 06 expired-options, 09 instrument-master) from the operator's tickvault `dhan-ref/*` mirror + DhanHQ public docs + the pasted Expired-Options page: base `https://api.dhan.co/v2/`; **Data API 5 r/s · 100,000/day**; auth `access-token` (JWT, 24h) + `dhanClientId` via TOTP `auth.dhan.co/app/generateAccessToken` (no daily manual click → §11 AUTOMATED holds); **`/charts/intraday`** (intervals 1/5/15/25/60, **90-day/call**, 5-yr depth, `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`) + **`/charts/historical`** (daily, to inception); **columnar parallel-array** response (vs Groww row-arrays) → parser transposes → `CandleBar`; **prices RUPEES → ×100 paisa** at the parse boundary; timestamps UNIX-epoch-UTC; instrument master `images.dhan.co/api-data/api-scrip-master-detailed.csv` keyed by numeric **`SECURITY_ID`** (HARD rule: never hardcode — replaces Groww's `groww_symbol`). **SPOT/OPTIONS SPLIT (operator clarification, same day):** `/charts/intraday` + `/charts/historical` are **SPOT UNDERLYING ONLY** (3 brute-force indexes + INDIA VIX reference + 200-stock spot); **ALL option OHLC is pulled EXCLUSIVELY from `/charts/rollingoption`** (expired-options rolling, ATM±10 index / ±3 other, 30-day/call, 5-yr) — because in a backtest every contract is already expired. The intraday `expiryCode`/`oi` fields are therefore unused by Track 1. **Atomicity:** Groww is wired into the core (cli.py ingest driver; dashboard pull/instrument/universe flow; `config/credentials.py`; the `groww_symbol` field in `data/watermark.py`; `costs/types.py` `Broker` literal; `quota_tracker.py`), so the strip + Dhan rebuild are **one migration delivered as a sequence of individually-green commits** (additive Dhan adapter first, then the atomic cli/dashboard/schema flip + Groww deletion) per the Merge-Readiness Charter (CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 13) — never a half-broken intermediate. **Unchanged:** the brute-force engine (3 indexes + VIX-reference, Rule 3), data lake, feed/resampler, exec sim, `brutex.money`, audit hash-chain, adaptive token bucket, work-item state machine, and `adapters/protocol.py` (`MarketAdapter`) — all vendor-neutral by design (§11 COMMON). **Reinforces** DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339): Dhan reconstructs each minute from the official exchange feed (the data-fidelity motivation), and Track 1 still ingests **1m-only** + resamples. **Phase 0 HARD-GATE (Golden Rule #1/#10):** index `SECURITY_ID`s (NIFTY/BANKNIFTY/SENSEX) + INDIA VIX presence are **NOT in the docs** — resolve from the CSV + live probe before any production pull; flagged UNVERIFIED in each `dhan-ref/*` file, not asserted. **Installment 1 (this entry):** `docs/dhan-ref/` + ADR 0002 + this decision — **spec/ADR before code** (mirrors how ADR 0001 shipped as Phase 0a, no adapter code); additive, CI-green. Remaining installments (adapter package, cli/dashboard/schema flip + Groww deletion, CI/pyproject purge, doc reconciliation) land as follow-on green commits on `claude/automated-merge-ready-pipeline-IpP85`. **Builds on / supersedes:** ADR 0001 / DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 · DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339) · DEC-P0-063 (options lake) · CLAUDE.md §1 (scope) / §2 Rules 1·2·3·6·11·13 / §6 (vendor facts) / §10 (honest limits).

342. **DEC-EXTREME-PERF-AUTOMATION-001 — standing operator decree: ultra-fast O(1)-per-operation everywhere + comprehensive real-time automated checks, across every layer** (operator 2026-05-31: "irrespective of any situations … frontend backend db or it can be anything always i need ultra fast extreme speed to achieve O(1) uniqueness deduplication O(1) latency with O(1) time complexity and O(1) space complexity by covering all worst cases situations scenarios errors exceptions bugs issues … comprehensively fully extremely automated along with real time checks with guarantee and assurance"). **This reinforces, it does NOT change, the existing charter** — CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1) per hot path: bar lookup, indicator lookup, combo dedup Bloom+LMDB, u64 mask eval, result append; CI gate `p99(N=1M)/p99(N=1K) ≤ 1.1`), §11 SCALABLE (same code path Day-1→Phase-10), and §12 (throughput canary ≥ 50 M/s + 4-tier dedup + real-time observability). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10 — the load-bearing caveat):** "O(1)" is the binding target **per operation** (lookup / dedup / mask-eval / append / write), which is real and CI-enforced; it is **NOT** literally achievable for **total space** — ingesting years of 1-minute bars is inherently O(n) storage, and a full brute-force sweep is O(combos). So the guarantee is: **O(1) per-element hot-path time + bounded/streaming per-op space + linear aggregate scaling**, never "O(1) total." Any claim of "O(1) space complexity" for whole-dataset operations is rejected here as physically impossible (Golden Rule #1). **Scope:** applies to every surface (adapter, lake, feed, exec, engine, orchestrator, dashboard/frontend, DuckDB/LMDB/Parquet, CI/CD) — each new module's PR must show its hot-path O(1) bench + worst-case/error-path tests, or state honestly why a path is exempt. **Builds on:** CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 / §11 / §12 · DEC-P0-001/003 (vulture/jscpd/mutation hard gates) · the Merge-Readiness Charter (Rule 13, priority law Correctness > Reliability > Recoverability > Observability > Performance > Scalability — performance never above correctness).

343. **DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 — Dhan ingest pulls 1-minute ONLY for both spot AND options, plus 1-day for spot anchors; every other timeframe is resampled** (operator 2026-05-31: "for this dhan also our idea is to pull always only one day data and only one min data alone for the entire spot … and for options also only one min data"). **Extends DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339) from Groww to Dhan, and now explicitly to OPTIONS.** The ingest fetch set is: **SPOT** (3 brute-force indexes + INDIA VIX reference + 200-stock spot) → `/charts/intraday` `interval="1"` (1-minute) **only**, plus `/charts/historical` daily (**1-day**, for PDH/PDL anchors); **OPTIONS** → `/charts/rollingoption` `interval="1"` (1-minute) **only**. Dhan's other native intraday intervals (5/15/25/60) are **never fetched** — the 19 derived intraday timeframes are produced by **deterministic resampling from the 1m base** (`brutex/feed/resampler.py`), preserving the single-source-of-truth + bit-exact-resample guarantee. **Rationale (carried from #339):** only the 1-minute series is a faithful minute-by-minute record reconstructed from the official exchange feed; native higher-TF aggregations are untrusted and re-derivable, so fetching them wastes quota and risks drift. **Effect on the chunk planner:** the Dhan `chunks` module iterates a 1-element interval set for the spot intraday pull (mirrors Groww's `ALL_GROWW_INTERVALS = ("1m",)`), `interval="1"` for the options pull, with the 90-day (spot intraday) / 30-day (options) windows from `docs/dhan-ref/05` + `06`. `docs/TRACK1_DHAN_ADAPTER.md` + `docs/dhan-ref/05,06` updated to lock the options-1m rule. **Builds on:** DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339) · DEC-GROWW-DHAN-REVERT-001 (#341) · CLAUDE.md §1 (1m base + deterministic resample) / §2 Rule 6 (rate-limit/quota thrift).

344. **DEC-DHAN-DERIVED-TF-LADDER-001 — all 19 derived intraday timeframes are resampled from the 1m base for SPOT UNDERLYINGS ONLY; the exact ladder is `2..15` (each minute) + `30m,1h,2h,3h,4h`** (operator 2026-05-31: "based on only one min data alone only we need to derive all other timeframes … that too one and only for spot underlyings alone … using one min timeframe all other timeframes should be derived starting 2 till 15 mins sequentially incrementing one by one and 30mins and 1hr,2h,3h,4h"). **CONFIRMS the existing canonical model — it does NOT change it (verified against `brutex/data/timeframes.py`, Golden Rule #1):** `ALL_TF_MINUTES` already = `INTRADAY_MINUTE_TFS (1..15)` + `INTRADAY_MULTIMINUTE_TFS (30, 60, 120, 180, 240)` + `DAILY_TF_MINUTES (375)` = **21 TFs = 1m base + 19 derived intraday + 1d**, with `TF_LABELS` `1m,2m,…,15m,30m,60m,120m,180m,240m,1d`. The operator's "2 till 15 + 30m + 1h/2h/3h/4h" is exactly the 19 derived set (14 single-minute steps `2..15` + 5 multi-minute `30/60/120/180/240`). Resampling lives in `brutex/data/tf_resampler.py` (`resample_1m_to_tf` over `ALL_TF_MINUTES`, anchored to the 09:15 IST open per DEC-RESAMPLE-ANCHOR-001) — **bit-exact, deterministic, O(1)-per-bar streaming** (DEC-EXTREME-PERF-AUTOMATION-001 #342). **SPOT-ONLY scope-lock (the new constraint):** the derivation runs **only for spot underlyings** (3 brute-force indexes + INDIA VIX reference + 200-stock spot). **Options are 1-minute ONLY with NO resampling** (DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 #343) — option contracts are not aggregated to higher TFs in Track 1. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** `brutex/feed/resampler.py` carries a *separate*, Groww-era `RESAMPLEABLE_INTERVALS` set using native interval strings (`2minute,5minute,…`) that does NOT enumerate the full 1..15 ladder — a known pre-existing dual-resampler inconsistency; the **canonical** model is `data/timeframes.py` + `data/tf_resampler.py` (matches this decision). Reconciling/retiring the `feed/resampler.py` set is a tracked follow-up in the Dhan-adapter installment, not silently assumed done. **Builds on:** DEC-CANONICAL-1M-SOURCE-001 (#339) · DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · DEC-RESAMPLE-ANCHOR-001 · CLAUDE.md §1 (21 TFs: 1m base + 19 derived + 1d).

345. **DEC-DHAN-INSTRUMENTS-001 — Dhan scrip-master parser + universe selection; full 31-column fidelity (nothing dropped)** (operator 2026-05-31: "clearly note all nse indices and bse sensex and all other fno stocks spot alone pull it from dhan … dont even miss anything like lot size tick size freeze quantity nothing … refer it from tickvault code"). The **complete verified column set** (31 cols) was extracted from the operator's actual `api-scrip-master-detailed` file and locked in `docs/dhan-ref/09`: `EXCH_ID, SEGMENT, SECURITY_ID, ISIN, INSTRUMENT, UNDERLYING_SECURITY_ID, UNDERLYING_SYMBOL, SYMBOL_NAME, DISPLAY_NAME, INSTRUMENT_TYPE, SERIES, LOT_SIZE, SM_EXPIRY_DATE, STRIKE_PRICE, OPTION_TYPE, TICK_SIZE, EXPIRY_FLAG, BRACKET_FLAG, COVER_FLAG, ASM_GSM_FLAG, ASM_GSM_CATEGORY, BUY_SELL_INDICATOR, BUY_CO_MIN_MARGIN_PER, BUY_CO_SL_RANGE_MAX_PERC, BUY_CO_SL_RANGE_MIN_PERC, BUY_BO_MIN_MARGIN_PER, BUY_BO_PROFIT_RANGE_MAX_PERC, BUY_BO_PROFIT_RANGE_MIN_PERC, MTF_LEVERAGE, SM_UPPER_LIMIT, SM_LOWER_LIMIT, SM_FREEZE_QTY` — including the operator-named **`SM_FREEZE_QTY` (freeze quantity)**, `LOT_SIZE`, `TICK_SIZE`, price bands. **`brutex/adapters/dhan/instruments.py`** (this installment) is the pure parser: `parse_instrument_row` maps a CSV row → vendor-neutral `brutex.models.Instrument` (carries lot/tick/freeze/expiry/strike/underlying; `SECURITY_ID` → both `exchange_token` and `vendor_symbol`); `select_universe` does the **two-pass brute-force universe = all NSE indices + BSE SENSEX (+ INDIA VIX) + every F&O-eligible single-stock SPOT** (an EQUITY whose symbol appears as a derivatives `UNDERLYING_SYMBOL`); derivative legs parsed-for-eligibility then dropped from the lean universe (DEC-INSTRUMENT-UNIVERSE-FAST-001), recoverable on demand. **Storage layout locked** (`docs/dhan-ref/09` "Tables"): `instruments` DuckDB catalog (full 31-col fidelity, `SECURITY_ID` BIGINT PK → O(1)) + spot-bars Parquet tree + expired-options-bars Parquet tree (per DEC-EXTREME-PERF-AUTOMATION-001 #342). **Honest envelope:** `Instrument` (the engine model) carries the trading-relevant subset; the **catalog table** persists the full row so "nothing is missed." Mechanism mirrors tickvault's instrument loader (operator-confirmed reference), adapted to BruteX's model/lake. Parser is 100% line+branch + strict gate. **Builds on:** DEC-GROWW-DHAN-REVERT-001 (#341) · DEC-INSTRUMENT-UNIVERSE-FAST-001 · CLAUDE.md §1 (universe) / §11 COMMON.

346. **DEC-SPOT-PCT-CHANGE-001 — spot-only derived %-change features: precise previous-day-close % + a 09:15-IST open-gap %** (operator 2026-05-31: "for spot always … calculate precise prev day close percentage change along with … pre market open percentage change also which is at 9.15 am alone only … this is applicable only for spot"). **Two derived features, SPOT UNDERLYINGS ONLY** (3 brute-force indexes + INDIA VIX reference + 200-stock spot — **NOT options**): (1) **PDC % change** — `(close − prev_day_close) / prev_day_close × 100`, computed precisely against the prior trading day's daily close (the `/charts/historical` 1d anchor); (2) **open-gap %** — `(open_0915 − prev_day_close) / prev_day_close × 100`, computed **once at the 09:15:00 IST opening bar only** (the pre-market/opening gap). **Implementation site:** the spot **feature/resampler layer** (off the 1m + 1d lake), NOT the adapter — it is a derived computation after spot bars land, O(1) per bar (one prior-close lookup), full-precision (NOT money-rounded — it's a statistical metric, CLAUDE.md §4). Stored alongside the resampled spot TFs / stamped on the bar feature set. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this is a LOCKED spec decision; the code lands in a later installment once spot ingest exists (the adapter installments precede it). VIX is reference-only and not part of brute-force combos (CLAUDE.md §3), but the %-change is still computed + stamped for it as a spot series. **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · DEC-DHAN-DERIVED-TF-LADDER-001 (#344) · CLAUDE.md §4 (no rounding of statistical metrics) / §7 (09:15 IST open).

347. **DEC-SESSION-FILTER-001 — the lake holds ONLY regular-session bars: 09:15:00 IST inclusive → 15:30:00 IST exclusive; NO pre-open / post-close data ever** (operator 2026-05-31: "i should never ever have any pre market data or post market data … only between 9.15 am inclusive between 3.30 pm exclusive … only for one day no timestamps will be applicable … how will you filter this also while merging or pulling"). **Rule:** every **intraday** spot bar is filtered to ``[09:15:00, 15:30:00)`` IST at the **pull/ingest boundary** (before the lake write), so pre-open (09:00–09:15 auction) and at/after-close bars **never enter the lake**. A bar's UTC timestamp is converted to IST and kept iff its time-of-day is in the window — the 09:15–15:30 IST session maps to the same UTC calendar date (03:45–10:00 UTC), so no day rollover. **`1-day` bars are NOT time-filtered** (a daily bar has no intraday time — "for one day no timestamps applicable"). **Implementation:** vendor-neutral `brutex/data/session.py` (`in_session`, `filter_intraday_session`, `SESSION_OPEN=09:15`, `SESSION_CLOSE=15:30`), reused by every write path (CLAUDE.md §11 COMMON); wired into `brutex/adapters/dhan/ingest.py` (`ingest_spot` filters intraday windows, skips daily). O(1) per bar (one tz-convert + comparison; DEC-EXTREME-PERF-AUTOMATION-001 #342). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #1/#10):** the 09:15–15:30 hours are the CLAUDE.md §7 verified NSE/BSE regular session; the close-exclusive boundary means a 15:30:00 bar (if a vendor emits one) is dropped and the last kept 1m bar is 15:29. Holidays/half-days are handled by the no-data watermark (a non-session day returns nothing), not this time-of-day filter. Both modules 100% line+branch + strict gates. **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · DEC-RESAMPLE-ANCHOR-001 (09:15 anchor) · CLAUDE.md §7 (09:15–15:30 IST hours; pre-open excluded) / §11 COMMON.

348. **DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 — the locked Dhan pull scope: 2021-01-01 start + the options rolling-fan-out (ATM±10 indices / ATM±3 stocks, CE+PE, WEEK+MONTH × all 4 expiryCodes, 1-minute, mapped to the underlying spot)** (operator 2026-06-01: "pull it from starting 2021 January … for options … from starting Jan 2021 using only one min … all atm plus minus of ten especially for indices and for stocks plus or minus atm plus or minus 3 … of its ce pe weekly monthly … mapped with its respective underlying symbol and it's spot", confirmed via 3-question scope lock). **Two locks:** **(1) Date floor → 2021-01-01.** The Track-1 **operational** pull floor moves from Dhan's vendor capability (2020-01-01, `DhanMarketAdapter.earliest_history`; CLAUDE.md §6) to **2021-01-01** for the safer case (the 2020 edge's quality is unverified) — exactly CLAUDE.md §3's locked "Rolling 2021-01-01 → today" range. Implemented this installment: `pull_controller.DHAN_HISTORY_FLOOR = date(2021, 1, 1)` (the dashboard-pull clamp) + `cli.cmd_fetch` defaults its start to that floor so a bare `brutex fetch` pulls the full locked history. **(2) Options rolling-fan-out spec (driver lands next installment).** For every option series: **1-minute only** (DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001); **strike window** = ATM±10 for the brute-force **indices** (21 moneyness offsets) and ATM±3 for the **F&O stocks** (7 offsets); **both CE + PE** (the `/charts/rollingoption` per-side payload returns both in one call); **WEEK + MONTH** expiry flags × **all 4 expiryCodes** (the previously-locked DEC-P0-063 depth — near+next+next2+next3 per flag); **mapped to the underlying** — the rolling endpoint resolves strikes relative to the underlying's spot and **stamps `spot` + resolved `strike` on every bar** (already built in `dhan/option_ingest.py` / `option_parser.py`). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #1/#10):** BANKNIFTY weeklies were discontinued after 2024-11-20 (entry #103/#133) and NSE single-stock options have no weeklies — a WEEK request there simply returns an empty side that the parser **skips gracefully (no error, no bad data)**, so the fan-out can request WEEK uniformly. The exact `expiryCode` 0–3 semantics (near/next/…) are verified against the Dhan rolling-option body builder when the fan-out driver lands; the options ingest is **LAKE-only / REFERENCE_ONLY** — the BRUTE-FORCE ENGINE still sweeps only the 3 indices (CLAUDE.md §3; DEC-P0-063). **Builds on:** DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 · DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · DEC-P0-063 (#200, the 4-expiryCode options lake) · DEC-SESSION-FILTER-001 (#347) · CLAUDE.md §3 (2021→today range) / §6 (vendor depth) / §11 COMMON.

349. **DEC-CANARY-MINTIME-001 — throughput-canary ratio gates use best-of-N (min) timing to kill the chronic shared-GHA flake (no threshold downgrade)** (2026-06-01, resolving the repeated `Benchmarks (O(1) NFR gates)` flake that twice blocked unrelated PRs). **Problem:** `tests/bench/test_throughput_canary.py`'s ratio gates (`test_vectorized_mask_and_ratio`, `test_dict_bar_lookup_ratio`, `test_evaluate_atoms_ratio`) timed each side (`t_small`, `t_large`) with a SINGLE sample, so one shared-runner scheduler-jitter slice could skew the ratio past the ceiling even though the op is genuinely O(1) — the chronic flake DEC-P0-020 + PR #46 fought by bumping the ceiling 5× → 8× (which still tripped). **Fix:** each side is now timed `_TIMING_REPEATS = 7` times and the **MIN** is taken — the minimum is the least-jitter-contaminated estimate of the true per-element/per-call cost (textbook micro-bench practice, cf. `timeit.repeat → min`). **The ratio threshold is UNCHANGED (8× / dict 10×) — this is NOT a downgrade** (operator decree "100%, don't downgrade"): a real O(n)/O(n²) regression still lands at 30-100× and is caught; only the noise is removed. Verified: the 3 ratio canaries pass 5/5 consecutive local runs (was intermittently failing 1-in-N on GHA). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the absolute-throughput gates (`test_throughput_absolute`, `test_instrument_bulk_upsert`) remain self-hosted-M4-Pro-only (DEC-P0-020); this fix is for the always-on RATIO canaries. **Builds on:** DEC-P0-020 (#42, ratio-not-absolute), CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1) per hot path) / §12 (throughput canary).

350. **DEC-FETCH-COVERAGE-001 — O(1) fetch-window coverage ledger: re-pull skips already-pulled windows so no Dhan hit is ever wasted** (operator 2026-06-01: "whenever we pulled the proper precise working response from dhan then we should not pull the same data back again … how will you keep track … not to waste the dhan hit limits"). **Rule:** the spot ingest tiles `[start, end)` into fetch windows (one window = one vendor API call); `brutex/data/fetch_coverage.py` (`FetchCoverage` + `WindowKey`) records each **completed** window in an **O(1)** hash set keyed by `(market, security_id, interval, start, end)`. `ingest_spot` / `run_dhan_spot_ingest` now take an optional `coverage` ledger: a window already marked complete is **skipped before the vendor call** (no wasted Dhan hit — the Data API ceiling is **5 req/s**, `docs/dhan-ref/01`), and a window is marked complete after a successful fetch + append. The window key includes the exact `(start, end)`, so the **current** (today-touching) window gets a fresh key each day → never wrongly skipped on the next day's pull, while every **settled past** window is skipped. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this is a **quota/latency** optimisation layered on the existing correctness backstop — the idempotent sink already makes a re-pull safe (no duplicate/corruption, CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 7), so skipping can never lose data; it only avoids re-burning the Dhan budget on data already in the lake. `FetchCoverage` serialises (`to_records`/`from_records`) for cross-run persistence (the DuckDB/lake-backed persistence + the options-fan-out coverage are tracked follow-ups; this installment ships the spot tracker + the `ingest_spot`/`run_dhan_spot_ingest` skip wiring at 100% line+branch). **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348) · DEC-COVERAGE-LEDGER-001 (the universe/expiry ledger) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1) dedup) / Rule 6 (rate-limit safety) / Rule 7 (idempotence).

351. **DEC-DHAN-EXPIRY-CODES-001 — the rolling-option expiry codes are `1, 2, 3` (Near / Next / Far), NOT 4** (operator 2026-06-01, with the Dhan Annexure + live `Send API Request` 200-OK probes pasted as the verified source). **Finding:** the `/charts/rollingoption` endpoint documents `expiryCode` **Allowed values: `1`, `2`, `3`** (1-indexed for this endpoint); the general **Annexure → Expiry Code** indexes the same three depths as `0` = Current/Near, `1` = Next, `2` = Far (0-indexed). So Dhan serves **three** expiry depths, not four. **Correction:** `option_universe_ingest.DEFAULT_EXPIRY_CODES` changes `(0, 1, 2, 3)` → **`(1, 2, 3)`** — the endpoint's documented Near/Next/Far. This **supersedes the unverified "all 4 expiryCodes" wording** of DEC-P0-063 (#200) + DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348), which was a Groww-era carryover ("4 Groww expiryCodes") never confirmed for Dhan. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #1/#10):** no data was lost by the prior default — it still captured all three depths via `1/2/3`, and the per-series quarantine made the stray `0` harmless — but it wasn't doc-faithful and wasted one API call per series on the undocumented `0`; the `(1,2,3)` set is doc-exact + quota-frugal. The operator's live probe also showed `expiryCode 4` returns `200 OK` with data (undocumented far+1); it is deliberately **excluded** (not in the endpoint's Allowed values) — addable later if a deeper-than-Far depth is wanted. `expiry_codes` stays an injected parameter, so any future set is a one-line caller override. **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348) · DEC-P0-063 (#200) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 1 (no hallucination) / Rule 6 (rate-limit safety). Source: `https://dhanhq.co/docs/v2/annexure/#expiry-code` + `https://dhanhq.co/docs/v2/expired-options-data/` (operator-pasted 2026-06-01).

352. **DEC-DHAN-ADAPTIVE-RPS-001 — adaptive [3..5] r/s Dhan rate controller: start at 5, step down 5→4→3 under heavy 429s, recover 3→4→5, never above 5 / below 3** (operator 2026-06-01: "dhan stated max 5 rps per second we need to follow that but … only when … heavy rate limit then decrement to 4 then … decrement to 3"). **`brutex/adapters/dhan/rate_control.py::DhanRateController`** runs the pull at Dhan's verified **5 r/s** Data ceiling (`docs/dhan-ref/01`) when healthy and only steps **down 1 r/s** on *sustained* rate-limiting (`STEP_DOWN_HITS=3` hits within `STEP_DOWN_WINDOW=60 s`), recovering **up 1 r/s** after a `STEP_UP_WINDOW=300 s` clean window — clamped to `[FLOOR_RPS=3, CEILING_RPS=5]` (the floor is a **clamp, not a halt**; asymmetric fast-down / slow-up). It owns only the small Dhan-specific step ladder; the **O(1) blocking pace** + the daily-quota gate are **reused** from `AdaptiveTokenBucket` (CLAUDE.md §11 COMMON — rebuilt at the new rate on each rare step, sharing the same `DailyQuotaTracker` so the day's count never resets). All hooks (`pace` / `on_rate_limit` / `on_success`) are O(1) (a bounded deque + comparisons). **Reconciliation (Golden Rule #10):** this **supersedes the "5 × 0.6 = static 3 r/s" wording** of CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 6 / §6 (a Groww-era safety baseline) — the safety intent survives as the **floor (3)**, not the start; the **5 r/s hard ceiling** is unchanged. It aligns with `docs/dhan-ref/01`'s already-"adaptive [3..5]" policy text. **Honest envelope:** this installment ships the **controller core + tests** (100% line+branch + strict gate); the **wiring** (threading `pace` / the 429→`on_rate_limit` + clean→`on_success` feedback through `ingest_spot` / `option_ingest` + the live `_build_live_spot_adapter`) is the **next** PR — until then the Dhan fetch path is still unthrottled (the limiter is plumbed-but-not-yet-fed, the same honest gap the design surfaced). **Builds on:** DEC-P0-020 (#42, ratio gates) · DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 · DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1)) / Rule 6 (rate-limit safety) / §6 (verified Dhan limits) / §11 COMMON.

353. **DEC-DHAN-OPTIONS-PULL-CONTROLLER-001 — the options pull controller: one `pull_options(...)` core behind the four options dashboard buttons (② bulk indices-options, ④ bulk stocks-options, ⑥ per-index, ⑧ per-stock)** (operator 2026-06-01: the 8-button matrix "{bulk, per-symbol} × {indices, stocks} × {spot, options}" — this installment is the **options** backend, the spot side having landed as `pull_all_spot` / `pull_candles`). **`brutex/dashboard/option_pull_controller.py`** mirrors the spot `pull_controller` (CLAUDE.md §11 COMMON — same offline-first shape, same `[2021-01-01, today]` window reused from `resolve_candle_range`): a single `pull_options(instruments, *, lake_root, dry_run, now, expiry_flags, expiry_codes, option_types, client_factory)` maps each resolved `Instrument` to an `OptionUnderlying` via `instrument_to_option_underlying` (**IDX → `OPTIDX` / ATM±10**, **EQ → `OPTSTK` / ATM±3**, `exchange → {NSE,BSE}_FNO`; a blank `vendor_symbol` or a non-IDX/EQ leg raises), then drives ONE `run_dhan_options_ingest` fan-out (ATM-window × WEEK/MONTH × `DEFAULT_EXPIRY_CODES=(1,2,3)` × CALL/PUT) so the live client is built **once** and a terminal-erroring series is **quarantined** without sinking the rest. A one-element list is the per-symbol button; the full universe is the bulk button; an empty list is a clean no-op. The `OptionsPullReport` aggregates per-underlying `series_completed` / `series_failed` / `bars_appended` / `windows` + a `to_dict()` for the (next-PR) `/api/v1/universe/pull-options` route. **Offline-first:** the default + CI-tested path is dry-run (`SyntheticOptionClient` + `NullOptionBarSink`) — the whole fan-out → parse → session-filter → sink pipe runs with no token / no network; the live `_build_live_options` (`DhanClient` + concrete `OptionBarSink` over `OptionDedupIndex` + `OptionParquetWriter`) is `# pragma: no cover` (real creds + network, blocked in CI). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** (a) the live path threads only the rate controller's `pace` (the 5 r/s ceiling); the rolling-option spine does **not** yet accept the 429-feedback hooks (`on_rate_limit`/`on_success`), so the adaptive 5→4→3 step-down for options is a **tracked follow-up** — until then live options hold at the safe ceiling; (b) the routes + the live-dashboard button wiring + the options fetch-coverage ledger are the **next** PRs; this installment ships the controller core at **100% line+branch** (+ strict per-module CI gate). **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348) · DEC-DHAN-EXPIRY-CODES-001 (#351) · DEC-DHAN-ADAPTIVE-RPS-001 (#352) · DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 · DEC-DHAN-1M-ONLY-001 (#343) · DEC-P0-063 (#200, options LAKE-only) · DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-001 · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1)) / Rule 6 (rate-limit safety) / Rule 7 (idempotence) / §3 (scope) / §11 COMMON.

354. **DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-ROUTES-001 — the dashboard pull routes wiring all 8 buttons to their backends + the pure bulk-target selector** (operator 2026-06-01: "bulk pull for indices spot and bulk pull for indices expired options and bulk pull for stocks spot and bulk pull for stocks expired options and each symbol …" — the 8-button matrix gets its HTTP surface). **Two pieces:** **(1) `brutex/dashboard/universe_pull.py::select_pull_instruments(snapshot, *, kind, fno_eligible)`** — the **pure** core that returns the concrete `Instrument` objects (not the grid's JSON rows) to bulk-fan a pull over for one explorer kind (`index` → every admitted `IDX`; `stock` → every F&O-eligible `EQ`), sorted by `trading_symbol` for a deterministic order, **reusing the single COMMON classifier** `universe_query.classify_instrument` (CLAUDE.md §11 — the bulk pull selects exactly the universe the grid shows). 100% line+branch + strict per-module CI gate. **(2) Two FastAPI routes in the coverage-omitted `app.py`** (the live-wiring layer, exercised manually like the existing `/pull` route): `POST /api/v1/universe/pull-all?kind=index|stock` → `pull_all_spot` (buttons ① ③, bulk spot 1m+1d over `[2021-01-01, today]`), and `POST /api/v1/universe/pull-options` (buttons ② ④ ⑥ ⑧) → `pull_options` — `?symbol=…` fans one underlying (per-symbol ⑥/⑧), else `?kind=…` bulk-fans the whole kind (② ④); exactly one of `symbol`/`kind` is required (else 400). Both routes force `dry_run=True` when Dhan creds are absent (offline preview, mirroring `/pull`) and surface the **real** failure reason on error (DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001), never a blank 400. **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** the routes themselves are coverage-omitted wiring (same standing rationale as `app.py`/`cli.py`); their non-trivial logic — the target selection — is extracted into the 100%-covered pure `select_pull_instruments`. The **live-dashboard button HTML wiring + animated SSE progress** (replacing the mockup) and the **options fetch-coverage ledger** remain the next PRs; this installment ships the route surface + the tested selector. **Builds on:** DEC-DHAN-OPTIONS-PULL-CONTROLLER-001 (#353) · DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348) · DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-001 · DEC-INSTRUMENT-UNIVERSE-FAST-001 · DEC-PULL-ERROR-VISIBILITY-001 · CLAUDE.md §11 COMMON / §3 (scope).

355. **DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-BUTTONS-001 — the animated live "Pull the Universe" panel: all 8 buttons wired into the real `universe.html`** (operator 2026-06-01: "we will have dashboards … by clicking simple spot instruments we need to pull data manually … pull all indices spot data … meanwhile entire stocks spot symbols should also be pulled … it should be real time live dashboard where even millions of users should be always easily attracted and impressed"). The served `/universe` page (`brutex/dashboard/templates/universe.html`) gains a top **"⚡ Pull the Universe" hero** with the 4 animated bulk buttons (① Indices·Spot, ② Indices·Options, ③ Stocks·Spot, ④ Stocks·Options — shimmer + glow-pulse, per-surface colours) POSTing the merged routes `/pull-all?kind=…` / `/pull-options?kind=…`, plus a per-underlying **"⛓ Pull options chain"** button (⑥/⑧) next to the existing spot pull (⑤/⑦). All paths animate the shared progress panel (beam + count-up + live stat counters: symbols / series / option-bars) and stamp the **PREVIEW · synthetic** badge when `dry_run`. **Vendor-text cleanup:** the lingering Groww strings in this template (cred banner, empty-state, date-picker tooltips + comments) are corrected to **Dhan** — env vars `BRUTEX_DHAN_ACCESS_TOKEN` / `BRUTEX_DHAN_CLIENT_ID` (verified `brutex/adapters/dhan/auth.py`), history floor `2021-01-01` (DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this is a **frontend template** (HTML/CSS/JS) served verbatim by the coverage-omitted `app.py`; it is not Python-unit-tested (templates carry no coverage) — its backends are the 100%-covered controllers + the merged routes. The result panels render **one-shot completion totals** (the synchronous report), not yet a streaming token-by-token feed; the true **SSE per-window live progress** is a tracked follow-up. **Builds on:** DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-ROUTES-001 (#354) · DEC-DHAN-OPTIONS-PULL-CONTROLLER-001 (#353) · DEC-DHAN-PULL-SCOPE-001 (#348) · DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-001 · DEC-VENDOR-PIVOT-001 · CLAUDE.md §3 (scope) / §11 COMMON.

356. **DEC-PULL-PROGRESS-TRACKER-001 — the O(1) thread-safe live pull-progress tracker (foundation for the real-time SSE feed)** (operator 2026-06-01: "it should be real time live dashboard … always easily attracted and impressed", + the standing O(1)/real-time decree). **`brutex/dashboard/pull_progress.py::PullProgress`** is the shared progress state a background pull worker pushes to and the live dashboard route reads from: a strict state machine `idle → running → done|error` with O(1) mutations (`start` / `advance` / `finish` / `fail`) and an O(1) `snapshot() → ProgressSnapshot` (immutable, `to_dict()` for the JSON/SSE route). **Every mutation + read is O(1)** — a handful of integer writes under a single `threading.Lock` (no scans, no per-unit allocation; CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5) — so the tracker never bottlenecks a 200-symbol universe pull, and the lock makes worker-thread writes + reader-request reads **torn-update-free** (worst-case concurrency covered by a 10-thread × 100-advance test asserting zero lost updates). `start` resets the same instance so a re-click is idempotent (CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 7); `completed` clamps to `total` and `percent` guards the zero-total divide. The clock is injected for deterministic timestamps. **100% line+branch + strict per-module CI gate.** **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this installment ships the **tracker only** (the pure, tested foundation); threading `on_progress` through the spot/options ingest drivers + the SSE / polling route + the dashboard JS that consumes it are the **next** PRs — until then the dashboard still shows the synchronous one-shot totals (DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-BUTTONS-001). **Builds on:** DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-BUTTONS-001 (#355) · DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-ROUTES-001 (#354) · DEC-EXTREME-PERF-AUTOMATION-001 (#342) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1) per hot path) / Rule 7 (idempotence) / §11 COMMON.

357. **DEC-PULL-PROGRESS-WIRING-001 — `on_progress` threaded through the spot + options ingest drivers and the bulk controllers (the backend half of the live feed)** (operator 2026-06-01: "real time live dashboard … real time guarantee and assurance"). Adds an optional **`ProgressHook = Callable[[str, int, bool], None]`** to `run_dhan_spot_ingest` (fires once per `(symbol, interval)` pair → `(label, bars_appended, failed)`; a quarantined pair fires `bars=0, failed=True`) and to `run_dhan_options_ingest` (fires once per **underlying** after its whole fan-out → `(security_id, bars_appended, any_failed)`). The bulk controllers thread it through: `pull_all_spot` + `pull_candles` (spot) and `pull_options` (options) all gain an optional `on_progress` that forwards to the driver, so a caller can wire it to `PullProgress.advance` (DEC-PULL-PROGRESS-TRACKER-001 #356) for a real-time per-unit feed. **O(1):** the hook is one call per processed unit (no extra scan); when `None` (every existing caller) the path is unchanged — proven by the untouched existing suites still green. **All four modules stay 100% line+branch** (new success/failure-branch tests added to each strict-gated suite). **Honest envelope (Golden Rule #10):** this is the **backend half** — the per-unit progress now *emits*; the **SSE / polling route + the dashboard JS** that subscribe to a per-request `PullProgress` and tick the counters window-by-window are the **next** PR. Until then the dashboard still renders the synchronous one-shot totals (DEC-UNIVERSE-DASHBOARD-BUTTONS-001). **Builds on:** DEC-PULL-PROGRESS-TRACKER-001 (#356) · DEC-UNIVERSE-PULL-ROUTES-001 (#354) · DEC-DHAN-OPTIONS-PULL-CONTROLLER-001 (#353) · CLAUDE.md §2 Rule 5 (O(1)) / §11 COMMON.
